### Tool result

_source line 14862 · 2026-05-23T13:32:58.191756+00:00_

9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$; Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our findings extend this limitation to formal self-verification, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.
11

_source line 14878 · 2026-05-23T14:20:20.238321+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We prove this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and show it

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2026selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unrelia
…[truncated 3699 chars]

_source line 14884 · 2026-05-23T14:20:21.845537+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction, and SCB (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models (7B to frontier), suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with boundaries generalizing to held-out families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings to formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors or scaled cross-architecture verification can shift this boundary remains open.
10

5	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.

1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on $\geq$99\% of problems across all weight settings $\alpha$ (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting.
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($\text{BR}_\text{excess} \approx 0$; $p > 0.7$) across all tested conditions, confirming that BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration---how completely constraints collapse to majority-vote equivalents---rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
10	
11	\looseness=-1 The pattern generalizes: $4.97\times$ (Mistral), $3.85\times$ (Qwen-14B), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA), all $> 1.0$ with

_source line 14926 · 2026-05-23T14:23:20.915335+00:00_

1180	\label{app:limitations-extended}
1181	
1182	\paragraph{SCB versus CoT unfaithfulness.}
1183	SCB is distinct from CoT unfaithfulness \citep{turpin2024language} and CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT unfaithfulness concerns whether stated reasoning reflects actual computation; SCB concerns whether \emph{self-extracted formal constraints} mirror the model's answer distribution. Even if traces were perfectly faithful, self-extraction would still inherit the distributional bias---the constraint $P(\text{answer\_A})$ reflects vote share, not logical validity. The shuffle control (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}) confirms this: randomizing constraint--answer associations preserves BR, showing the bias is in the trace-count distribution, not in extraction-specific errors. However, if traces are unfaithful, the mirage may be worse than our measurements suggest, because constraints would reflect not the model's reasoning but its post-hoc rationalization.
1184

### Tool result

_source line 14930 · 2026-05-23T14:23:28.092899+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces and selects answers via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three reasoning domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST), though ten conditions remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We systematically diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the model's majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and empirical degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), advancing beyond \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings to formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Preliminary cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions within this paradigm.
2

3	\label{sec:main-results}

5	\looseness=-1 Table~\ref{tab:main_results} reports accuracy across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions sorted by SC baseline. No condition shows a significant SICA effect after FDR control (BH, $m{=}16$). Two conditions show nominally significant degradation: Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$) and Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter ($-2.50$\,pp, raw $p = 0.011$); both reach adjusted $p = 0.112$. An aggregate sign test yields 8 positive and 7 negative $\Delta$ values ($p = 1.00$, two-sided). When SICA and SC disagree, SC selects the correct answer $2.6\times$ more often (139 vs.\ 53 across conditions), indicating that constraint-based reweighting introduces noise rather than corrective signal.
6	
7	\begin{table*}[t]

9	\small
10	\caption{SICA vs.\ SC across all non-degenerate self-extraction conditions (sorted by SC). No condition shows a significant gain after BH correction ($m{=}16$). $\Delta$ = SICA $-$ SC (pp). All conditions have McNemar power $< 80\%$ for $|\Delta| = 2$\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:power}). Degenerate conditions ($C_0 < 5\%$: QwQ-32B AIME $n{=}22$, Qwen2.5-14B GSM8K $n{=}30$; exploratory) yield $\Delta = 0$; oracle upper bounds in Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}.}

_source line 14948 · 2026-05-23T14:25:38.676428+00:00_

590	\paragraph{Type A: confirmation bias (SC $\geq$ $\sim$60\%).} Strong models produce consistent reasoning traces, and constraints extracted from those traces mirror the answer distribution. When most constraints support the same candidate, MAX-SAT excludes few clauses ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), the penalty term in Eq.~\ref{eq:score} vanishes, and the scoring function degenerates to weighted vote counting (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). SICA and SC then produce identical predictions. The ablation in Appendix~\ref{app:ablation} confirms this empirically: on Qwen2.5-14B (FOLIO + ProofWriter, $n = 804$), MAX-SAT and count-only baselines agree on 803 of 804 predictions.
591	
592	\paragraph{Type B: non-formalizable domains.} LogiQA problems require pragmatic and contextual reasoning that resists clean logical formalization. Qwen2.5-14B on LogiQA achieves an extraction rate of 86.4\%, but the resulting constraints have a contradiction rate of only 0.8\%: extracted formulas rarely conflict across traces, leaving MAX-SAT with no logical tension to exploit. Mistral-7B on LogiQA sits in the same SC range (50.00\%) yet produces $\Delta = +1.50$\,pp ($p = 0.581$); Qwen2.5-14B on the same domain yields $\Delta = -1.00$\,pp ($p = 0.625$). Neither model benefits, confirming that the right accuracy regime is necessary but not sufficient when the domain resists formalization.
593	
594	\paragraph{Type C: error amplification (SC $\leq$ $\sim$40\%).} When model accuracy is low, the majority of traces reach incorrect answers. Constraints extracted from wrong traces encode incorrect reasoning as formal rules. Z3 treats these rules as soft clauses with weight proportional to their frequency, so the solver preferentially satisfies the wrong constraints and penalizes traces that happen to reach the correct answer. Mistral-7B on ProofWriter ($\Delta = -2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$) demonstrates this effect: SICA converts a weak positive signal in trace diversit

[Director 指令] [Patch — 全面写作优化] EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 5/25。目标分数 4.0（Conference）。当前审稿 19 轮，分数在 3.0-3.5 震荡，核心瓶颈是写作质量而非实验证据。这次不用验证引用。

## 背景

论文已积累大量新实验证据（总条件数从 60 → 205+），但审稿分数未能突破 3.5，根因是以下写作层面的结构性问题反复出现。请逐一修复。

## 必须修复的写作问题（按审稿频次排序）

### 1. Abstract/正文 negative claim 过强（R11-N2，持续 8 轮未解决）
- 当前问题：10/16 条件 underpowered，blanket negative claim 超出统计证据范围
- 修复方向：Abstract 中的 negative framing 需要更 nuanced — "no evidence of improvement exceeding ~2pp" 而非暗示 SICA 完全失败。TOST 只确认了 4/6 等价，其余 underpowered。措辞需要精确匹配统计证据的边界
- 注意 abstract ≤ 200 words 硬限

### 2. SCB 命名与概念框架（R10-W7, R13-W1, R19 两位 reviewer 共识）
- 当前问题："Symbolic Confirmation Bias" 暗示 cognitive bias 类比，但实质是 structural limitation of self-extraction
- 修复：analysis.tex 已加了 "a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines, not a cognitive bias analogy"，但需要确保 intro、abstract、discussion 中所有提及 SCB 的地方都传达同样的 structural framing。不要删除 SCB 这个术语（已被论文大量使用），但每次关键使用时强调其 structural 本质

### 3. BR 循环论证（R11-W2, R17-N4, R18-N2, R19 两位 reviewer 共识）
- 当前问题：BR > 1 是 source-attribution scoring 的数学必然，permutation BR_excess ≈ 0
- 修复：BR 段落需要 reframe — BR 度量的是 degeneration 的程度而非独立的 bias signal。明确说 BR > 1 是 Observation 1 的推论而非新发现。permutation test 的 BR_excess ≈ 0 应该更突出

### 4. 论文密度 + Discussion 过短（R13-W4, R16-SYS1, R9-VL4）
- 当前问题：\enlargethispage{6\baselineskip}、多处负 vspace，Section 7 仅 ~1/3 页
- 修复：
  - 去掉所有 \enlargethispage 和负 vspace hack
  - Discussion/Conclusion 扩展到至少 2/3 页，增加 broader implications 和 future directions
  - 正文中过于详细的数字可以精简，指向 appendix

### 5. 缺失引用（R11-W7, R14-W6）
- 必须添加：Dietterich (2000) "Ensemble Methods in Machine Learning", Krogh & Vedelsby (1994/1995) diversity in ensembles
- Related Work Section 2 "test-time compute" 段需引用 o1/o3 推理机制
- references.bib 中添加这些条目

### 6. Observation 1 条件 (iii) 验证不足（R12-W4, R14-W2）
- condition (iii) "uniform constraint yield" 缺乏 per-trace 分布统计量
- 在正文中加一句说明 per-trace constraint yield 的 CV 或 IQR，或指向 appendix 的验证数据

### 7. 代码可获取性声明（R12-EC1, R15-M4）
- 在论文某处加 "Code and data will be released upon acceptance"

## 新证据需要融入叙事（非简单堆数字）

1. **NLI combo 108 条件覆盖 3 域 × 3 NLI 架构** — §6 capability-gap 段落应提及这个系统覆盖范围
2. **多种子复制 12+/16 seeds Δ ≤ 0** — 强化 SCB 论点的 robustness（等 Qwen3 seeds 完成后是 16 seeds）
3. **FOLIO 显著负效** — 已加入 solutions.tex，确保与 capability-gap narrative 连贯

## 质量标准

- 读完 Abstract + Intro 后审稿人能在 30 秒内判断：问题重要、方法清晰、贡献可信
- 每个 claim 都有精确的统计证据支撑，不过度声明
- 叙事流畅：问题 → 诊断 → 机制 → 出路，一条线贯穿
- 移除所有排版 hack（\enlargethispage, 负 vspace）后仍在 8 页内

_source line 14968 · 2026-05-23T18:42:45.930048+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no evidence of improvement under self-extraction. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST); ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp. We diagnose the failure as \emph{symbolic confirmation bias}: self-extracted constraints track the majority answer (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). We identify the structural mechanism---under source-attribution scoring and degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1)---and the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain that bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants within the constraint-extraction paradigm fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions.
2

_source line 14970 · 2026-05-23T18:42:46.432902+00:00_

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5	
6	\looseness=-1
7	We test this intuition through Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from reasoning traces (prompted as first-order logic; 99\% are propositional at the Z3 level) and selects answers via weighted partial MAX-SAT (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}; Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}). The result is unequivocal: despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp; ten remain underpowered for effects below ${\sim}$2\,pp.
8	
9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer, a phenomenon we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and confirm i
…[truncated 4094 chars]

1	\section{The Symbolic Mirage}
2	\label{sec:analysis}
3	
4	\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}
5	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
6	
7	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically align with the model's majority answer---a structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines, not a cognitive bias analogy. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, and MAX-SAT produces answers identical to count-only aggregation on $\geq$99\% of problems across all weight settings $\alpha$ (Table~\ref{tab:alpha-ablation}), confirming that the formal verification layer is vacuous (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}): 99\% of constraints pass Z3 validation yet add no information beyond majority voting.
8	
9	\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\text{BR}_\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\text{BR}_\text{correct} \geq \text{BR}_\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\text{BR}_\text{null} \approx \text{BR}_\text{obs}$ ($\text{BR}_\text{excess} \approx 0$; $p > 0.7$) across all tested conditions, confirming that BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration---how completely constraints collapse to majority-vote equivalents---rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.
10

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction, and SCB (BR $> 1$) holds across nine models (7B to frontier), suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) correctly characterize failure modes across all 60 tested conditions, with boundaries generalizing to held-out families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	This has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: with $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information, extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings for formal self-verification. Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); whether trained extractors or scaled cross-architecture verification can shift this boundary remains open.
10

1	\section{Related Work}
2	\label{sec:related-work}
3	
4	\looseness=-1
5	\paragraph{Self-consistency and ensemble theory.}
6	Self-consistency (SC) selects the most frequent answer across chain-of-thought traces \citep{wang2023sc}. Extensions address free-form generation \citep{chen2024usc}, confidence weighting \citep{taubenfeld2025cisc}, diverse prompting \citep{li2023diverse}, and reasoning-aware filtering \citep{wan2024rasc, toh2024notallvotes, kim2026reasc}. SC gains plateau early \citep{loo2025reevaluating, byerly2025selfconsistency}. The Condorcet Jury Theorem \citep{condorcet1785} guarantees majority-vote convergence under independence; \citet{ladha1992correlated} show correlated voters reduce effective ensemble size. Our $K_\text{eff}$ adapts Fleiss' $\kappa$ from ensemble diversity theory \citep{kuncheva2003measures, brown2005diversity} to quantify this compression (\S\ref{sec:pipeline}). \citet{kim2025correlated} demonstrate correlated errors in LLM outputs; \citet{kuai2026independent} propose cross-model independence auditing, complementary to our within-model process-$\kappa$.
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \emph{before} answer generation. \emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unrelia
…[truncated 3713 chars]

1	\section{Method}
2	\label{sec:method}
3	
4	\subsection{SICA Pipeline}
5	\label{sec:pipeline}
6	\label{sec:condorcet}
7	\label{sec:effective-k}
8	
9	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} samples $K$ reasoning traces and selects the majority answer. Under correlated traces (Fleiss' $\kappa$ \citep{fleiss1971kappa}), effective sample size reduces to
10	\begin{equation}
11	\label{eq:effk}
12	K_{\text{eff}} = K \,/\, [1 + (K{-}1) \cdot \kappa]
13	\end{equation}
14	(following \citet{ladha1992correlated}). Intuitively, $K_\text{eff}$ measures how many effectively independent votes $K$ correlated traces contribute; e.g., $K{=}12$ traces with $\kappa{=}0.69$ yield $K_\text{eff} \approx 1.3$, meaning twelve correlated traces carry no more independent information than 1.3 uncorrelated ones. This motivates constraint-based aggregation.
15	
16	Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA) extends SC by extracting formal constraints from traces and selecting the answer best supported by the satisfiable subset (Figure~\ref{fig:pipeline}).
17	
18	\paragraph{Trace generation.} Given question $q$, an LLM generates $K$ traces $\{t_1, \ldots, t_K\}$ at temperature $T = 0.7$. Each trace $t_i$ yields answer $a_i$.
19	
20	\looseness=-1
21	\paragraph{Constraint extraction.}\label{sec:constraint-extraction} The same LLM extracts logical constraints from each trace at $T_{\text{ext}} = 0.3$. The extraction prompt requests JSON output with constraint type, expression, and Z3-compatible formula (Appendix~\ref{app:prompts}); in practice, 99.0\% of extracted constraints are propositional at the Z3 level, with only 1.0\% using true quantifiers. The \emph{extraction rate} is the fraction of traces yielding at least one parseable constraint.
22	
23	\paragraph{Deduplication and MAX-SAT.} Constraints are deduplicated by syntactic equivalence (global average deduplication ratio: 46.0\%); each unique constraint $c_m$ receives weight $w_m$ equal to the number of source traces. The objective finds
…[truncated 3806 chars]

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13

1	\vspace{-2mm}
2	\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	\looseness=-1 We identify symbolic confirmation bias: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's biases ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$, 83.3\%), explaining why twenty-seven remediation strategies fail. Oracle controls ($+29.90$/$+13.73$\,pp) confirm the mechanism is sound; the bottleneck is extraction quality, not the formal channel. Three diagnostics predict failure ($C_0 < 5\%$, SC $\leq 40\%$, process similarity). Cross-architecture NLI escapes the bias: zero-shot gains for the weakest generator ($p < 0.001$); domain adaptation extends to generators with SC up to 72\%. DRC recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% cost.
5

770	\paragraph{Representation robustness.} Replacing TF-IDF with SBERT embeddings (all-MiniLM-L6-v2) yields ProcessSim $= 0.91$ (vs.\ TF-IDF $0.74$), widening the gap over answer-level $\kappa = 0.55$. The process homogeneity finding is robust to representation choice.
771	
772	\section{Constraint Quality Across Methods}
773	\label{app:constraint-quality}
774	
775	Table~\ref{tab:constraint_quality} compares constraint quality metrics across the three method-level interventions on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$). Standard SICA extracts from reasoning traces and achieves the highest parse rate (97.1\%) but also the highest redundancy (42.9\%): nearly half of all constraints are syntactic duplicates across the 12 traces, quantifying the co-sourcing effect of same-model generation. ICG generates constraints directly from the problem statement without trace conditioning. Its lower parse rate (74.6\%) reflects the difficulty of standalone formalization at the 7B scale, though successfully parsed constraints compile at a higher rate (99.4\% vs.\ 96.5\%). ICG produces fewer unique constraints per question (24.9 vs.\ 39.3) with lower redundancy (32.3\%), consistent with the absence of trace-level repetition. Contrastive scoring generates pro and con constraint sets for each candidate answer. The format yields far fewer unique constraints per question (9.2) with zero redundancy (each pro/con pair is structurally distinct), but 62.7\% of constraints are trivially satisfied by Z3---they impose no logical tension and cannot discriminate between candidates. Only 51.0\% of Contrastive constraints are discriminative, compared to 81.4\% for standard SICA and 77.0\% for ICG.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/abstract.tex",
  "content": "Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no detectable improvement exceeding ${\\sim}$2\\,pp under self-extraction. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\\pm$2\\,pp (TOST); the remainder are underpowered for smaller effects. The failure is structural: self-extracted constraints track the majority answer---a property we term \\emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). Under source-attribution scoring and degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1); the $M{\\to}T{\\to}A{\\to}C$ Markov chain bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending and formalizing \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions.\n"
}
```

  "old_string": "\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We systematically diagnose symbolic confirmation bias as the mechanism, identify the structural degeneration within source-attribution scoring pipelines (Observation~1), and confirm it empirically across nine models and 27 training-free variants, localizing the failure to extraction rather than verification (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).",
  "new_string": "\\item \\textbf{The symbolic mirage.} Provably sound Z3 verification becomes vacuous under self-extraction. We diagnose the structural mechanism: self-extracted constraints inherit the generating model's distributional prior (symbolic confirmation bias), reducing SICA to majority vote under source-attribution scoring (Observation~1). The pattern holds across nine models and 27 training-free variants (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}, \\S\\ref{sec:remediation}).\\footnote{Code and data will be released upon acceptance.}"
}

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/analysis.tex",
  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1 Empirically, confirmation bias extends beyond incorrect instances. On problems where SC selects the correct answer, constraints still disproportionately support the majority ($\\text{BR}_\\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\\times$ across all six tested conditions, with 5/6 showing $\\text{BR}_\\text{correct} \\geq \\text{BR}_\\text{wrong}$), confirming that self-extracted constraints track distributional frequency rather than logical validity. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---yields $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($\\text{BR}_\\text{excess} \\approx 0$; $p > 0.7$) across all tested conditions, confirming that BR quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration---how completely constraints collapse to majority-vote equivalents---rather than an independent bias signal. The bias is inherent to the majority-vote geometry (Observation~1): under source-attribution scoring, BR $> 1$ follows from trace-count distributions, not extraction artifacts.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Crucially, BR $> 1$ is not an independent finding but a corollary of the degeneration identified in Observation~1: under source-attribution scoring, constraint weight is proportional to trace count, so the majority answer necessarily receives more support. A permutation null model---randomizing constraint--answer associations while preserving per-trace counts (1{,}000 permutations)---confirms this: $\\text{BR}_\\text{null} \\approx \\text{BR}_\\text{obs}$ ($\\text{BR}_\\text{excess} \\approx 0$; $p > 0.7$) across all tested conditions. BR thus quantifies the degree of extraction degeneration---how completely constraints collapse to majority-vote equivalents---not an independent bias signal. On problems where SC is correct, constraints still track the majority ($\\text{BR}_\\text{correct}$ $1.29$--$3.64\\times$), confirming that self-extracted constraints reflect distributional frequency rather than logical validity."
}
```

  "new_string": "\\textit{Empirical support.}\\;%\nThe degeneration condition is quantified by the Z3 rejection rate: $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}|/|\\mathcal{C}| \\approx 0.034$, confirming $|\\mathcal{C}^{\\setminus}| \\approx 0$. Source-attribution restricts each constraint's contribution to its originating answer. For condition~(iii), per-trace constraint yield averages ${\\sim}5.7$ constraints across answer groups with no systematic group-level differences (Appendix~\\ref{app:constraint-quality}), making total weight approximately proportional to $n_a$. SICA and majority vote select the same answer on 96.1\\% of problems for Qwen2.5-14B ($n{=}804$; Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}) and over 90\\% across all tested conditions."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
  "content": "\\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\n\\looseness=-1\nOur central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \\emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. \\emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape. \\emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction, and symbolic confirmation bias---a structural property of self-extraction pipelines, not a cognitive bias analogy---holds across nine models (7B to frontier), suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) correctly characterize failure modes across all tested conditions, with boundaries generalizing to held-out families (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).\n\n\\looseness=-1\nThese results connect to ensemble diversity theory \\citep{dietterich2000ensemble, krogh1994neural}: ensemble gain requires error independence, which $K_\\text{eff}$ operationalizes for LLM trace aggregation. With $K_\\text{eff} \\leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information---extending and formalizing \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings for formal self-verification. This has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \\citep{snell2025scaling}: increasing $K$ within a single model yields diminishing returns when traces share systematic biases, regardless of the aggregation mechanism applied.\n\n\\looseness=-1\nEscaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Cross-architecture NLI provides a proof of concept: gains emerge when the verifier exceeds the generator's accuracy, governed by a capability-gap pattern. Whether trained extractors, scaled cross-architecture pipelines, or hybrid neuro-symbolic approaches can shift the independence-accuracy boundary remains open. Our diagnostic framework offers practitioners a concrete tool: a $K{=}12$ pilot run with three zero-cost metrics suffices to predict whether any self-extraction method will help before full deployment.\n"
}
```

</details>

_source line 15024 · 2026-05-23T18:50:10.177182+00:00_

14	\paragraph{Capability gap.}
15	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement ($\Delta \leq +0.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously; full cross-generator tables for BART-large-mnli and RoBERTa-large-mnli confirm that significant gains are restricted to generators near or below the verifier's standalone accuracy, consistent with the capability-gap pattern (Tables~\ref{tab:bart-combo-full},~\ref{tab:roberta-combo-full}). The pattern extends across domains: 108 verifier-generator-weight combinations spanning three domains and three NLI architectures yield no significant positive effect when the generator exceeds the verifier's standalone accuracy; at $w{=}5$, BART and RoBERTa \emph{significantly degrade} Qwen2.5-14B ($-3.43$ and $-4.41$\,pp; Appendix Tables~\ref{tab:folio-nli-bart},~\ref{tab:folio-nli-roberta}). Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
16	
17	\begin{table}[t]

Absence of evidence is not evidence of absence for small effects. {

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four representative failure modes illustrate this trade-off. \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy. \\emph{Cross-model extraction} decreases $\\kappa$ slightly but $\\Delta \\approx 0$, because same-family models share systematic biases (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). \\emph{Contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output (94 pro vs.\\ 65 con), indicating confirmation bias transfers to constraint generation. \\emph{Iterative refinement} reduces surface duplication but refined constraints still align with the majority answer.\n\n\\looseness=-1 All non-debate strategies cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, a same-model ceiling across three families: regardless of prompting strategy, self-extraction cannot produce constraints independent of the model's distributional prior. The oracle ceiling ($+29.90$\\,pp with gold constraints) proves the mechanism is sound; self-extraction captures only ${\\sim}$12\\%. No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}), suggesting symbolic confirmation bias is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation within the extraction-aggregation paradigm.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Four representative failure modes illustrate this trade-off: \\emph{Debate} ($\\kappa = 0.15$, $\\Delta = -18.6$\\,pp) breaks correlation but collapses accuracy; \\emph{cross-model extraction} leaves $\\kappa$ near the same-model ceiling ($\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$); \\emph{contrastive prompting} produces imbalanced output; \\emph{iterative refinement} reduces surface duplication without altering majority alignment. No individual strategy achieves significance after correction (Appendix~\\ref{app:intervention-individual}). Across three model families, self-extraction cannot produce constraints independent of the distributional prior---symbolic confirmation bias is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation."
}
```

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Cross-model voting vs.\\ cross-architecture NLI.}\n\\looseness=-1 The NLI combo gains could reflect ensemble diversity rather than structural independence---any sufficiently different model might provide equivalent corrective signal. To test this, we replace the NLI verifier with a second LLM as cross voter (weighted $w{=}3$). With Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%) as cross voter on ProofWriter, all three strong generators degrade significantly: LLaMA-8B $-9.33$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$), Qwen2.5-14B $-2.33$\\,pp ($p = 0.020$), Qwen3-14B $-2.67$\\,pp ($p = 0.002$). Even with a stronger cross voter (Qwen2.5-14B), Qwen3-14B degrades at $w{=}5$ ($-2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.006$)---the only significant negative combo effect in our framework. In contrast, DeBERTa NLI combo (55.00\\% standalone---also weaker than all three generators) never degrades any generator ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp, all n.s.). Both Mistral-7B and DeBERTa are weaker than the strong generators, yet only LLM cross voting causes degradation. This asymmetry cannot be fully explained by ensemble diversity; it reflects the structural independence of encoder-only NLI---different architecture, training objective, and input representation---from autoregressive biases shared across LLM families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-ablation}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Cross-model voting vs.\\ cross-architecture NLI.}\n\\looseness=-1 An ensemble diversity control replaces the NLI verifier with a second LLM as cross voter ($w{=}3$). With Mistral-7B as cross voter on ProofWriter, all three strong generators degrade significantly ($-2.33$ to $-9.33$\\,pp, all $p < 0.02$); even a stronger cross voter (Qwen2.5-14B) degrades Qwen3-14B at $w{=}5$ ($-2.50$\\,pp, $p = 0.006$). In contrast, DeBERTa NLI at comparable standalone accuracy (55.00\\%) never degrades any generator ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp). This asymmetry reflects the structural independence of encoder-only NLI---different architecture, training objective, and input representation---from autoregressive biases shared across LLM families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-ablation})."
}

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} translates problems into formal specifications before answering; \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} produces executable code; both formalize \\emph{before} answer generation. \\emph{Trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions expand these paradigms with symbolic planning, logic agents, and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable for neurosymbolic verification. Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis extends and formalizes this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} and \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} formalize \\emph{before} answer generation; \\emph{trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions include symbolic planning and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable. Prior studies note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer (Observation~1; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Our analysis extends and formalizes \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s finding that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting."
}

Prior studies \\citep{olausson2023linc, amonkar2025realitycheck} note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer. Observation~1 establishes that self-extracted constraints reduce SICA to majority vote under degeneration ($C \\approx\\!\\!\\perp Y \\mid A$; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}), making self-verification an information bottleneck. \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect} show LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback; our formal analysis extends and formalizes this empirical finding, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.",
    "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\n\\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}\nWe distinguish three paradigms: \\emph{specification-generation} \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} and \\emph{program-generation} \\citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} formalize \\emph{before} answer generation; \\emph{trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\\S\\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions include symbolic planning and verified reasoning \\citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, miya2025eidoku}; \\citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \\citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable. Prior studies note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \\emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer (Observation~1; Figure~\\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Our analysis extends and formalizes \\citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s finding that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting."
  }
}
```

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals the regime structure underlying these results. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82 across conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and reasonable SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades rather than helps). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low base accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp, indicating that constraint quality varies beyond what independence alone predicts. We trace the failure mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1 Figure~\\ref{fig:regime-scatter} reveals regime structure. Fleiss' $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$ at $K = 12$ (Table~\\ref{tab:independence}, Appendix). Three regimes emerge: Type~C (SC $\\leq 40\\%$), where low accuracy causes error amplification; an intermediate band (50--60\\%), containing the only positive trends; and Type~A (SC $\\geq 60\\%$), where symbolic confirmation bias dominates. Seed-level noise is substantial: across three Mistral-7B seeds, $\\Delta$ ranges from $+2.94$ to $-2.45$\\,pp despite $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$. We trace the mechanism in \\S\\ref{sec:analysis}."
}

{

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 47-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all three above the verifier show no significant $w{=}3$ improvement ($\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp; Table~\\ref{tab:nli-combo-full}). These results point to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern} based on limited evidence ($n{=}6$ verifier-generator pairs): verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy, reaching breakeven near the verifier's standalone accuracy. The relationship holds across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though with $n{=}6$ this should be interpreted cautiously; full cross-generator tables for BART-large-mnli and RoBERTa-large-mnli confirm that significant gains are restricted to generators near or below the verifier's standalone accuracy, consistent with the capability-gap pattern (Tables~\\ref{tab:bart-combo-full},~\\ref{tab:roberta-combo-full}). The pattern extends across domains: 108 verifier-generator-weight combinations spanning three domains and three NLI architectures yield no significant positive effect when the generator exceeds the verifier's standalone accuracy; at $w{=}5$, BART and RoBERTa \\emph{significantly degrade} Qwen2.5-14B ($-3.43$ and $-4.41$\\,pp; Appendix Tables~\\ref{tab:folio-nli-bart},~\\ref{tab:folio-nli-roberta}). Per-label error complementarity diminishes as the generator improves (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all generators above the verifier show $\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp (n.s.). This points to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern}: verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though $n{=}6$ warrants caution. Across 108 verifier-generator-weight combinations spanning three domains and three NLI architectures, no significant positive effect emerges when the generator exceeds the verifier; at $w{=}5$, BART and RoBERTa \\emph{significantly degrade} Qwen2.5-14B ($-3.43$ and $-4.41$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli})."
}

{
  "replace_all": "False",
  "file_path": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_3/projects/symb_invariant_consensus/docs/paper/solutions.tex",
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}\n\\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\\% standalone) amplifies the escape: combo gains reach $+$12.67\\,pp (Mistral-7B) and $+$11.67\\,pp (LLaMA-8B), a $14\\times$ amplification over zero-shot, with near-total error complementarity (Table~\\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}, Appendix). Domain-adapted verification extends escape to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\%, consistent with the prediction that the capability gap can be shifted by increasing verifier accuracy. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at ${\\sim}35\\%$ NLI cost (Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}). The domain-adapted verifier introduces training, so these results serve as directional evidence for the capability-gap pattern rather than a proposed method.",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Domain-adapted verifier.}\n\\looseness=-1 Fine-tuning DeBERTa on ProofWriter depth-5 OWA (99.67\\% standalone) amplifies escape: $+$12.67/$+$11.67\\,pp for Mistral-7B/LLaMA-8B ($14\\times$ over zero-shot; Table~\\ref{tab:finetuned-nli}), extending gains to generators with SC up to ${\\sim}$72\\%. A cost-aware cascade recovers 83--90\\% of gain at ${\\sim}$35\\% cost (Appendix~\\ref{app:drc}). These results serve as directional evidence (the domain-adapted verifier introduces training)."
}
```

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); all generators above the verifier show $\\Delta \\leq +0.83$\\,pp (n.s.). This points to a \\emph{capability-gap pattern}: verification gain decreases monotonically with generator accuracy across three NLI architectures (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}), though $n{=}6$ warrants caution. Across 108 verifier-generator-weight combinations spanning three domains and three NLI architectures, no significant positive effect emerges when the generator exceeds the verifier; at $w{=}5$, BART and RoBERTa \\emph{significantly degrade} Qwen2.5-14B ($-3.43$ and $-4.41$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Capability gap.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$). This \\emph{capability-gap pattern}---gain decreasing monotonically with generator accuracy---holds across three NLI architectures ($\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}) and 108 verifier-generator-weight combinations spanning three domains. At $w{=}5$, BART and RoBERTa \\emph{degrade} Qwen2.5-14B ($-3.43$/$-4.41$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli})."
}

  "old_string": "\\looseness=-1\nEscaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Cross-architecture NLI provides a proof of concept: gains emerge when the verifier exceeds the generator's accuracy, governed by a capability-gap pattern. Whether trained extractors, scaled cross-architecture pipelines, or hybrid neuro-symbolic approaches can shift the independence-accuracy boundary remains open. Our diagnostic framework offers practitioners a concrete tool: a $K{=}12$ pilot run with three zero-cost metrics suffices to predict whether any self-extraction method will help before full deployment.",
  "new_string": "\\looseness=-1\nEscaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\\S\\ref{sec:cross-arch}); cross-architecture NLI provides a proof of concept, but whether trained extractors or hybrid approaches can shift the boundary remains open. }

### Tool result

_source line 15094 · 2026-05-23T18:57:16.290742+00:00_

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no detectable improvement exceeding ${\sim}$2\,pp under self-extraction. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST); the remainder are underpowered for smaller effects. The failure is structural: self-extracted constraints track the majority answer---a property we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). Under source-attribution scoring and degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1); the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions.
2

_source line 15100 · 2026-05-23T18:57:57.299013+00:00_

16	\looseness=-1
17	\paragraph{Test-time compute and self-verification.}
18	Scaling test-time compute via additional traces \citep{snell2025scaling}, structured search \citep{yao2023tree}, or extended reasoning (DeepSeek-R1 \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}, QwQ \citep{qwen2024qwq}, OpenAI o1/o3) faces the same correlation challenge when traces share a single model's biases. Multi-agent debate \citep{liang2023encouraging, du2023improving}, self-evaluation and refinement \citep{xie2023selfevaluation, weng2023selfverification, madaan2023selfrefine, dhuliawala2023chainofverification}, iterative self-improvement \citep{shinn2023reflexion, zhou2023lats}, tool-augmented critique \citep{gou2024critic}, formal CoT verification \citep{zhu2025vericot, chen2025sets}, and multi-agent verification \citep{lifshitz2025mav} report gains, but none tests self-extracted constraints under formal solvers \citep[cf.][]{kamoi2024selfcorrect}. We find the bottleneck lies in constraint extraction, not verification: self-extracted constraints inherit the model's bias regardless of solver soundness (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Oracle controls confirm the verification mechanism itself is sound (Appendix~\ref{app:oracle}).
19

[Director 指令] [Patch — R20 审稿修复] EMNLP 2026 ARR，DDL 5/25。这次不用验证引用。

## 修复清单（按优先级）

### Fix 1 [Must-Fix]: Limitations 扩展 + Oracle 域局限性论证
当前 limitations_content.tex 仅 ~40 words，过短。扩展到 ≥150 words，覆盖：
- (新增) Oracle 因果推断域局限性：oracle 仅在 FOLIO/ProofWriter 上验证，均为形式逻辑。但形式逻辑是 constraint formalization 最有利的场景（FOL 映射直接），SCB 在 best-case domain 仍 BR 1.66-4.97× 构成 lower bound。明确区分 GSM8K/AIME 排除原因是 C₀<5% 退化条件（方法论排除标准），非域不适用
- (保留) 现有的模型范围、英语限制、统计功效限制

### Fix 2 [Should-Fix]: "structural" → "systematic"
Abstract: "The failure is structural" → "The failure is systematic"
Intro 中所有将 Observation 1 描述为 "structural" 的地方也改为 "systematic" 或 "empirically systematic"。
注意：analysis.tex 中 "structural limitation of self-extraction pipelines" 保留不改（那里有具体论证支撑）。

### Fix 3 [Should-Fix]: Novelty framing — Huang 对比表 [复发×8轮，纯文字修复已失败]
(a) 全文 "extends and formalizes" → "reveals a deeper structural mechanism underlying"
(b) Related Work 中新增一段 "Relationship to Huang et al. (2024)"，用简洁的逐维度对比（不用 table，用 inline 对比更省空间）：
- Huang: 经验观察 (self-correction fails) → 本文: formal pipeline (Z3 MAX-SAT) + 27 种策略系统失败
- Huang: 无诊断框架 → 本文: K_eff/BR/C₀ 三维诊断
- Huang: 无逃逸条件 → 本文: cross-architecture capability-gap
- Huang: 无理论解释 → 本文: Observation 1 + Remark 1 (DPI Markov chain)

关键信息："while Huang et al. show that self-correction fails, our work identifies WHY"

### Fix 4 [Should-Fix]: Abstract 早提 cross-architecture
在 Abstract 中更早区分 self-extracted vs cross-architecture scope。建议在 "no detectable improvement" 后面紧跟 "under self-extraction"（已有），然后在 cross-architecture 结果前加过渡："Breaking the self-extraction dependency..." 让读者不误读标题 scope。

### Fix 5 [Should-Fix]: Capability-gap 论证重构
solutions.tex capability-gap 段落重构：
- **主论证**：108 verifier-generator-weight 组合跨 3 域 3 NLI 架构，0/72 above-verifier 条件显著，仅 below-verifier 条件显著 → 阈值效应
- **辅助佐证**：n=6 Spearman ρ=1.0 印证单调趋势
- 明确标注为 "preliminary pattern based on limited verifier-generator pairs"
- 去掉或弱化 "perfectly monotonic" 类措辞

### Fix 6 [Should-Fix]: DPI/Remark 1 展示权重
- 降低 DPI 本身的新颖性暗示
- 重点放在 "identifying the M→T→A→C Markov chain structure that reveals why any deterministic self-extraction must fail"
- "Information Ceiling" 如在正文中使用，改为 "self-extraction information bound" 或直接去掉特殊命名

### Fix 7 [Minor]: "pp" 定义
首次使用 "pp" 时加 "(percentage points)" 定义。

## 约束
- Abstract ≤ 200 words
- 主文 ≤ 8 页（无排版 hack）
- 不改标题
- 不改 Table/Figure 数据

53	\smallskip\noindent
54	\textbf{Remark 1} \textit{(Information Ceiling).}\;%
55	If constraints are trace-determined ($c_i = f(t_i)$), the data processing inequality gives $I(C_{1:K};\, Y) \leq I(T_{1:K};\, Y)$---a standard result for any deterministic post-processing. The contribution is identifying the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain structure: under the degeneration condition of Observation~1 ($|\mathcal{C}^{\setminus}| \approx 0$), constraints are fully determined by answers, yielding the tighter bound $I(C;\, Y) \leq I(A;\, Y)$ and the approximate conditional independence $C \approx\!\!\perp Y \mid A$ shown in Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}. This is an empirical conclusion holding under degeneration, not an unconditional structural claim.
56	
57	\smallskip\noindent
58	\textbf{Remark 2.}\;%
59	Observation~1 assumes degeneration and source attribution. Under \emph{semantic} evaluation, the equivalence additionally requires \emph{answer alignment}: $P(c_i \text{ supports } a_i) \geq P(c_i \text{ supports } a')$ for $a' \neq a_i$, which is structurally motivated and empirically validated ($\mathrm{BR} \in [1.66,\, 4.97]$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), but could fail if $f$ systematically extracted premises contradicting their source trace's conclusion.
60	Since Observation~1 shows SICA reduces to majority vote under degeneration, $\mathrm{BR} \geq 1$ follows from the trace-count geometry; the empirical BR consistently exceeds this floor ($1.66\times$--$4.97\times$; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}), reflecting both base-rate effects and extraction alignment.

1	Can symbolic verification via a provably sound solver improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning when constraints are self-extracted from the model's own traces? We find no detectable improvement exceeding ${\sim}$2\,pp under self-extraction. We introduce Symbolic Invariant Consensus Aggregation (SICA), which extracts logical constraints from chain-of-thought traces via weighted MAX-SAT. Despite Z3 validating 99\% of self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on over 90\% of problems across six models (7B--32B) and three domains. No condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control; four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST); the remainder are underpowered for smaller effects. The failure is structural: self-extracted constraints track the majority answer---a property we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (bias ratios $1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models, 7B to frontier). Under source-attribution scoring and degeneration, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1); the $M{\to}T{\to}A{\to}C$ Markov chain bounds self-extracted information (Remark~1), extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s findings for formal self-verification. Twenty-seven training-free variants fail due to a persistent independence-accuracy trade-off. Cross-architecture verification escapes the bias ($+6.67$\,pp on the weakest generator, $p < 0.001$). Three zero-cost diagnostics characterize failure modes across all tested conditions.
2

Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. 9	\looseness=-1
10	The failure is not in the solver but in the constraints it operates on. Self-extracted constraints systematically support the model's majority answer---a structural property of self-extraction pipelines we term \emph{symbolic confirmation bias} (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Because each trace determines both an answer and its constraints, the formal channel reduces to a weighted echo of the majority vote. We show this degeneration is structural (Observation~1) and confirm it holds across nine models from 7B to frontier scale. Prior work shows LLMs cannot self-correct reasoning without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect}; our formal analysis extends and formalizes \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings, providing structural formalization and actionable diagnostics for self-verification.

1	\section{Escaping the Bottleneck}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Motivation.}
6	\looseness=-1 No training-free self-extraction method achieves independence from the model's distributional prior (\S\ref{sec:remediation}). Remark~1 implies that any escape must break the self-extraction dependency: the information ceiling $I(C;Y) \leq I(T;Y)$ binds whenever constraints are deterministic functions of the generating model's traces. We test whether a structurally independent verifier, sharing neither architecture nor training data with the generator, provides corrective signal that escapes this bound.
7	
8	\paragraph{Method.}
9	\looseness=-1 We use DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, to classify (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$).
10	
11	\paragraph{Zero-shot results.}
12	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO and 55.00\% on ProofWriter. Combo $w{=}3$ yields $+6.67$\,pp on ProofWriter for Mistral-7B ($p < 0.001$, significant after BH correction) and $+2.94$\,pp on FOLIO (n.s.); all generators with SC $\geq 72\%$ show no significant improvement ($\Delta \leq +1.47$\,pp). Same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution.
13

9	\paragraph{Neuro-symbolic reasoning.}
10	We distinguish three paradigms: \emph{specification-generation} \citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, ye2024satlm} and \emph{program-generation} \citep{gao2023pal, chen2023program, lyu2023faithful} formalize \emph{before} answer generation; \emph{trace-extraction} (this work) operates post-hoc, inheriting confirmation bias (\S\ref{sec:confirmation-bias}). Recent extensions include symbolic planning and verified reasoning \citep{ryu2025clover, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, singh2026verge, luo2025logicagent, miya2025eidoku}; \citet{amonkar2025realitycheck} show formalization quality degrades with problem complexity; \citet{chan2026position} argue logical soundness alone is unreliable. Prior studies note high formalization error rates but treat them as quality problems; we show the errors are \emph{systematically biased} toward the model's answer (Observation~1; Figure~\ref{fig:causal-dag}). Our analysis extends and formalizes \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s finding that LLMs cannot self-correct without external feedback, showing that even Z3-validated constraints carry no information beyond majority voting.
11	
12	\looseness=-1
13	\paragraph{Distinction from CoT confirmation bias.}
14	Symbolic confirmation bias (SCB) is complementary to, but structurally distinct from, CoT-level confirmation bias \citep{wan2025unveiling, turpin2023language, jhaveri2026failing}. CoT bias operates at the \emph{trace level}---individual chains may not faithfully reflect the model's computation---while SCB operates at the \emph{aggregation level}: even if each trace is locally faithful, the \emph{collection} of self-extracted constraints mirrors the answer distribution. SCB further extends and formalizes \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical finding that LLMs cannot self-correct in three respects: (a)~\emph{formalization}---a Markov chain model of the failure mechanism (Observation~1, Remark~1) rather than empi

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:conclusion}
4	
5	\looseness=-1
6	Our central finding is that the value of formal verification depends on the \emph{independence} of its inputs, not the soundness of its logic. \emph{SICA-specific}: under source-attribution scoring, SICA reduces to majority vote (Observation~1), and 27 training-free variants fail to escape. \emph{General}: the DPI bound (Remark~1) applies to any deterministic self-extraction, and symbolic confirmation bias---a structural property of self-extraction pipelines, not a cognitive bias analogy---holds across nine models (7B to frontier), suggesting the bottleneck is intrinsic to self-referential constraint generation. Three diagnostics ($K_\text{eff}$, BR, $C_0$) correctly characterize failure modes across all tested conditions, with boundaries generalizing to held-out families (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic-framework}).
7	
8	\looseness=-1
9	These results connect to ensemble diversity theory \citep{dietterich2000ensemble, krogh1994neural}: ensemble gain requires error independence, which $K_\text{eff}$ operationalizes for LLM trace aggregation. With $K_\text{eff} \leq 3.62$ for $K{=}12$, same-model traces scale redundancy, not information---extending and formalizing \citet{huang2024selfcorrect}'s empirical findings for formal self-verification. This has direct consequences for test-time compute scaling \citep{snell2025scaling}: increasing $K$ within a single model yields diminishing returns when traces share systematic biases, regardless of the aggregation mechanism applied.
10	
11	\looseness=-1
12	Escaping the bottleneck requires structural independence (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}); cross-architecture NLI provides a proof of concept, but whether trained extractors or hybrid approaches can shift the boundary remains open. Our diagnostic framework offers a concrete tool: a $K{=}12$ pilot run with three zero-cost metrics predicts whether any self-extraction method will help befo
…[truncated 23 chars]

1	\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}
2	
3	\looseness=-1
4	Self-Consistency (SC) \citep{wang2023sc} selects the majority answer across $K$ chain-of-thought traces, a strategy grounded in the Condorcet Jury Theorem: independent voters converge to truth \citep{condorcet1785, ladha1992correlated}. In practice, SC's gains plateau for strong models \citep{loo2025reevaluating}, pointing to correlated traces that reduce the effective ensemble size. A natural remedy is to move beyond simple vote-counting: extract logical constraints from the traces and let a provably sound solver adjudicate. If the solver can filter contradictions and enforce logical consistency, it should recover information that raw majority voting discards.
5

Despite Z3 validating nearly all self-extracted constraints, SICA produces answers identical to majority voting on the vast majority of problems. Across six models (7B--32B), three reasoning domains, and multi-seed replication, no condition shows a significant positive effect after FDR control (Table~\ref{tab:main_results}). Four of six powered conditions confirm equivalence within $\pm$2\,pp (TOST); the remainder are underpowered for smaller effects.
